### Perlmutter LDMS/DCGM System Telemetry (NERSC, Performance Monitoring API)
*aka:* NERSC Perlmutter LDMS metrics; Performance Monitoring API telemetry; the telemetry corpus behind "Towards Application-Agnostic HPC Profiling" (SC25 poster)
*Org:* NERSC / Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory (LBNL); analysis co-led by University of Alabama (Bangalore group). LDMS tooling from Sandia National Laboratories  
*System:* NERSC Perlmutter (HPE Cray EX; NVIDIA A100 GPU nodes; HPE Cassini NIC over Slingshot 11 network)  
*Period:* Data collected via Perlmutter's always-on LDMS deployment; poster work circa 2024-2025 (SC25, Nov 2025). No fixed archival window - it is a continuous productio  
*Scale:* System-wide/production scale: Perlmutter has ~1,792 GPU nodes (4x A100 each). The poster works at per-job and system-wide granularity, mapping compute nodes to 
- **Contents:** Two telemetry planes. GPU side (NVIDIA DCGM counters, low-overhead): dcgm_gr_engine_active (graphics/SM engine active fraction, used to filter active samples), dcgm_fp64_active (double-precision FP activity), dram_active (memory activity), plus SM activity, FP64 compute activity, GPU memory bandwidth utilization, memory usage, and power consumption - aggregate and per-GPU. Network side (HPE Cassini NIC counters via LDMS): packets sent/received/total per interface, NIC utilization and imbalance percentages, and pause events (congestion signal). Derived products: FP64 roofline heatmaps (performance vs arithmetic intensity computed from fp64_active and dram_active), and a node-to-switch topolog
- **Failure info:** NONE. This is pure performance/utilization telemetry. It does NOT label failures or faults, does NOT expose job exit codes or return status, and does NOT provide root-cause fields. The only "attribution" it supports is performance-bottleneck attribution - deciding whether congestion (NIC pause events) originated at the job vs the shared Slingshot switch by comparing pause activity on job nodes vs neighboring non-job nodes on the same switch - which is a congestion-source inference, not a fault/failure diagnosis. There are no crash labels, no error codes, no anomaly/incident annotations. Any failure study would have to derive proxy signals (e.g., zero-activity GPUs, extreme power/thermal coun
- **Access:** Restricted / NERSC-internal. Not publicly released as a named archive or DOI. Access is gated behind a NERSC allocation (project m888-2025) and the Perlmutter Performance Monitoring API; there is no download link. The only public artifact is the SC25 poster describing the methodology: https://sc25.supercomputing.org/proceedings/posters/poster_files/post270s2-file3.pdf . Would require NERSC/LBNL co
- **Used by:** Primary: Hari Teja Jajula, Dhruva Kulkarni, Brian Austin, Purushotham V. Bangalore, "Towards Application-Agnostic HPC Profiling," SC25 poster (NERSC project m888-2025). Methodological lineage: Austin, Kulkarni, Cook, Williams, Wright, "System-wide Roofline Profiling: A Case Study on NERSC's Perlmutt
- **AI vs HPC:** Both, but with limits. The telemetry is workload-agnostic (its whole point is application-agnostic profiling), so it covers HPC simulation and AI/ML GPU workloads alike on the same A100 fabric - useful for comparing how AI vs traditional HPC jobs use GPU FP64/memory and stress the NIC. However it CA
- **Caveats:** (1) Not a packaged/released dataset - no DOI, no fixed schema, no row count; it is a live NERSC telemetry stream accessed via API, so reproducibility depends on NERSC allocation and retention. (2) No failure/fault/exit-code/root-cause labels at all - the single most important gap for failure research. (3) Known coverage gap acknowledged in the poster: LDMS did not collect packet-size-distribution

- **Contents:** Per-GPU DCGM hardware counters sampled every 10 seconds via LDMS (retrieved through the Prometheus API), merged with Slurm scheduler metadata (job ID, start/end times, allocated node lists), stored in Parquet. - **Access:** Not publicly released. No Zenodo/GitHub/DOI dataset artifact was found; the underlying NERSC operational monitoring data is internal. - **AI vs HPC:** Both (mixed HPC + AI workloads run on Perlmutter's GPU partition), but LOW usefulness for the "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why" question. - **Caveats:** Job-count figures differ between preprint versions (~75,703 post-filter vs ~345,154 cited elsewhere) — verify against the specific version. - **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only. ### Alibaba cluster-trace-v2017
*aka:* Alibaba clusterdata 2017; cluster-trace-v2017
*Org:* Alibaba Group (Alibaba Cluster Trace Program)  
*System:* Alibaba production cluster co-locating online services (long-running applications) and batch workloads  
*Period:* 2017 (July/August snapshot). 24-hour collection window with utilization/usage disclosed for only 12 consecutive hours.  
*Scale:* ~1,300 machines; ~11,101 online-service containers and ~12,935 offline/batch jobs. Six CSV tables: server_event, server_usage, container_event, container_usage,
- **Contents:** Machine layer: server_event.csv (machineID, timestamp, event type, event detail, CPU/mem/disk capacity) and server_usage.csv (CPU/mem/disk utilization, load averages). Online services: container_event.csv (create/remove events, instance_id, machine_id, resource requests, cpuset) and container_usage.csv (CPU/mem/disk %, load avg, plus CPI and MPKI performance counters). Batch: batch_task.csv (job_id, task_id, instance count, status, resource requests, create/modify timestamps) and batch_instance.csv (per-instance start/end time, machineID, status, retry/sequence counts, actual resource usage).
- **Failure info:** Partial failure labeling via status/event enums, but NO exit codes and NO systematic root-cause. (1) batch_instance status field takes: Ready | Waiting | Running | Terminated | Failed | Cancelled | Interrupted — so failed vs. successful (Terminated) instances are distinguishable, and retry-count fields are present. (2) batch_task status: Ready | Waiting | Running | Terminated | Failed | Cancelled. (3) Machine-level: server_event event type includes 'add' (machine available), 'softerror' (temporary unavailability), and 'harderror' (hardware failure), giving coarse machine-fault signal; any finer error reason can only be inferred from the free-text 'event detail' field. There are NO exit codes
- **Access:** Public. Repo/docs: https://github.com/alibaba/clusterdata/tree/master/cluster-trace-v2017 ; field docs at https://github.com/alibaba/clusterdata/blob/master/cluster-trace-v2017/trace_201708.md . Bulk data downloaded via links/scripts in the repo (README2017.md + SHA256SUM); no registration required.

Also cited in numerous cloud-scheduling / resource-management surveys (e.g., Investigation of Alibaba Cloud Data Set for Resource Management in Cloud Computing) as the first Alibab
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/cloud-only — general datacenter batch + online-service colocation, no GPU/DL-training workload. AI-only? No. It contains no ML/AI training jobs, so it cannot directly answer "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why." Its value to that question is only as a non-AI (traditional cloud batch) bas
- **Caveats:** Coarse and short: usage data covers only 12 hours; smaller and less detailed than v2018. Known data-quality gaps: ~15% of batch_instance rows are missing task_id/job_id; some container_usage instances have no matching container_event record. Failure semantics are label-only — the 'Failed'/'Interrupted' status and 'soft/harderror' machine events flag that something failed but never say why (no exit
- **Confidence:** high — dataset existence, tables, status

### Alibaba cluster-trace-v2018
*aka:* Alibaba clusterdata 2018; Alibaba production cluster data v2018
*Org:* Alibaba Group (Alibaba Cloud / Alibaba Open Cluster Trace Program)  
*System:* Production cluster running co-located Sigma (online container scheduler) and Fuxi (batch job scheduler)  
*Period:* 8 days (single continuous sampling window)  
*Scale:* ~4,000 machines; ~71K online service containers + ~4 million batch jobs. Six tables, ~48 GB compressed / ~280 GB uncompressed.
- **Contents:** Machine metadata + machine-level events (machine_meta); time-series machine resource utilization — CPU, memory, disk, load, network in/out (machine_usage); online-service container metadata and requested/limited resources (container_meta); container resource utilization time series (container_usage); batch job/task structure with plan_cpu/plan_mem, task_type, DAG dependency encoded in task_name (batch_task); per-instance runtime with cpu_avg/cpu_max/mem_avg/mem_max, machine_id, seq_no, and status (batch_instance). Finer-grained DAG dependency info than v2017 (e.g. task_name "M5_3_4" means task depends on tasks 3 and 4; hashed names are independent tasks).
- **Failure info:** PARTIAL — status/state labels only, no exit codes and no root cause. Both batch_task and batch_instance carry a `status` field. The documented instance state set is Ready | Waiting | Running | Terminated | Failed | Cancelled | Interrupted, where "Terminated" = successful completion and "Failed" = the try failed. Each batch_instance row is one try/run of an instance, so retries and a "Failed" outcome are observable, and the trace docs explicitly note an instance "may fail due to machine failures or network problems." However: (1) there is NO exit-code column and NO failure-cause/fault-type column — you get the outcome label, not why it failed; (2) machine_meta and container_meta also have `st
- **Access:** Public. Primary: GitHub https://github.com/alibaba/clusterdata (cluster-trace-v2018/trace_2018.md), download via provided Aliyun OSS links/script. Also mirrored on IEEE DataPort (open access): Kai Huang, "Alibaba production cluster data v2018," IEEE DataPort, 2024, doi:10.21227/cj2t-9698 — https://ieee-dataport.org/documents/alibaba-production-cluster-data-v2018
- **Used by:** Cheng et al., "Characterizing Co-located Datacenter Workloads in an Alibaba Warehouse-scale Cluster" / IEEE TCC work on co-located workloads; Liu & Yu, "Anomaly Analysis for Co-located Datacenter Workloads in the Alibaba Cluster" (arXiv:1811.06901); "Improving the cost efficiency of large-scale clou
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/cloud-datacenter side, NOT AI-workload-specific. For the "do AI workloads fail more than HPC, and why" question it serves only as a NO
- **Caveats:** Failure labels are outcomes only — no exit codes, no fault/root-cause taxonomy, no error messages. machine_meta and container_meta `status` values are undocumented in the public schema, so machine-failure events are essentially unusable as labels. Batch "Failed" conflates machine faults, network problems, and other causes indistinguishably. Known label/semantic quirk documented by users: many fail
- **Confidence:** high — dataset existence, scale, six-tab

*Org:* Alibaba (Alibaba Cloud / PAI team)

- **AI vs HPC:** AI-only (pure DL training + inference on a production GPU/MLaaS cluster; no classic HPC/MPI batch jobs). Partially useful for "do AI workloads fail more": it gives real AI-workload failure LABELS (Failed vs Terminated), so per-job/task failure rates are computable — but it offers no HPC counterpart 
- **Caveats:** Failure semantics are coarse: 'Failed' is a single opaque label with no exit code, error class, or root cause, and preemption/cancellation vs true faults are not separable. *Period:* Mustang: 27 Oct 2011 – 8 Nov 2016 (61 months, full machine lifetime). Trinity: ~3 months in 2017. Repository article published in USENIX ;login: Winter 2018.

- **Contents:** Per-job scheduler records. Trinity trace is a scheduler (MOAB/SLURM-style) log with analogous per-job timing, size, and status fields. There are NO explicit exit codes, NO root-cause or fault labels, and NO distinct SLURM FAILED / NODE_FAIL / OUT_OF_MEMORY states in the Mustang schema — non-timeout, non-cancelled failures are not separately distinguishable and effectively fold into the Completed/Cancelled buckets. Trinity's scheduler log carries a comparable set of terminal states but likewise exp
- **Access:** Public, no registration. - **Used by:** Amvrosiadis, Park, Ganger, Gibson, Baseman, DeBardeleben, "On the diversity of cluster workloads and its impact on research results," USENIX ATC 2018 (introduces the Mustang and Trinity traces). - **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only. Both LANL systems are traditional batch HPC (capacity computing on Mustang, capability computing on Trinity); no GPU/ML training workload and no AI-job labels. Useful as an HPC baseline for workload/job-outcome behavior, but its coarse three-state job_status (no exit codes, no fault root c
- **Caveats:** (1) Failure semantics are the main limitation — no exit codes, no NODE_FAIL/FAILED separation, no root cause; "Completed" means only "no reported error," so silent or hardware-induced failures are largely invisible. (2) Docs are v1.0beta; Trinity field list was inferred as analogous to Mustang rather than column-verified in this pass. (3) Two Sigma Datacenter A/B are listed but marked TBD/unreleas

### Failure Trace Archive (FTA)
*aka:* FTA; Grenoble/INRIA Failure Trace Archive (later hosted at University of Western Sydney)
*Org:* Created by INRIA (Grenoble, France) + TU Delft + Univ. of Western Sydney (UWS/Western Sydney Univ.). Authors: Bahman Javadi (UWS), Derrick Kondo (INRIA), Alexan  
*System:* 26 diverse parallel/distributed systems: HPC/SMP clusters (LANL lanl05, PNNL pnnl07), production grids (Grid'5000 g5k06, TeraGrid/NCSA teragrid06, Condor pools   
*Period:* Traces span 1994–2009 (individual durations range from 2 weeks e.g. ldns04/overnet03 to 9 years e.g. lanl05); archive published 2010 (CCGrid), expanded 2013 (JP  
*Scale:* 20 formatted data sets + 6 raw-only (26 total). Per-trace node counts range from ~40 (lri05/deug05) to 226,208 (sat09 SETI@Home) and 62,201 (ldns04 DNS servers)
- **Contents:** Resource-centric availability/failure traces in a standardized FTA format. Hierarchy Platform -> Node -> Component -> Event Trace. Core tables: platform, node, node_perf (benchmark performance), component (CPU/memory/disk/client-availability), creator (provenance/copyright), event_trace (event with start/stop times), event_state (state per event, e.g. CPU idle, host up/down). Plus code tables: component_type codes, event_type codes, and event_end reason codes. Each trace is an alternating time series of availability and unavailability intervals per resource. Distributed as raw, tab-delimited, and MySQL-dump formats, with a Matlab toolbox (initialize/query/process/finalize) and a GridSim-base
- **Failure info:** CRITICAL FIELD — mixed and mostly telemetry-level. The FTA models failure as availability vs unavailability (up/down) intervals of nodes/components; a failure = deviation from correct state, i.e. an unavailability interval. The FORMAT can carry richer labels via event_type codes (which component: CPU, memory, hard disk) and event_end reason codes (example values: 'disk crash', 'CPU overheating'), so component-level fault typing is representable. BUT for the MAJORITY of the 26 traces the data is only derived availability from external probing/benchmarking (hourly ping reachability for microsoft99; probe pings for overnet03/skype06/pl05/ldns04; HTTP fetches for websites02; CPU-idle benchmarks 
- **Access:** Public / open-access. Canonical site http://fta.scem.uws.edu.au/ (documentation, toolbox, downloads); mirror http://fta.cdms.westernsydney.edu.au/; original host was fta.inria.fr. NOTE: at check time (2026-07-08) both UWS/Western Sydney hosts returned connection-refused (137.154.148.23), so the site appears currently offline/intermittent — data may need to be retrieved via Web Archive (web.archive
- **Used by:** Foundational papers: Kondo, Javadi, Iosup, Epema, 'The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling Comparative Analysis of Failures in Diverse Distributed Systems,' CCGrid 2010, pp.398-407; Javadi, Kondo, Iosup, Epema, 'The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling the comparison of failure measurements and models of dis
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/distributed-systems ONLY; predates the AI/GPU-cluster era (traces 1994-2009, no ML/DL/GPU workloads). Provides the HPC/grid half of an AI-vs-HPC comparison: a cross-system baseline of NODE and COMPONENT hardware-failure and availability behavior for HPC clusters (LANL, PNNL), grids, desktop grid
- **Caveats:** (1) Coverage/label noise: only ~2 of 26 traces (lanl05, pnnl07) carry true failure root cause / component-level fault labels; the rest are probe- or benchmark-derived up/down availability with unavailability possibly caused by non-failure events (scheduled downtime, network partition, host simply going offline) — 'failure' here often means 'observed unavailable,' not confirmed fault. (2) No job ex
- **Confidence:** high — existence, authorship, format sch

*Period:* cae06, cs06 = 35 days (2006); glow06 = 33 days (2006); nd07cpu = 6 months (2007). (teragrid06, a sibling FTA grid trace from the same submitters, spans 8 months

- **Contents:** Resource-centric availability time series. Condor pools (cae06/cs06/glow06): alternating host available / unavailable intervals recorded by Condor. nd07cpu: time-stamped CPU load and idle-time samples per desktop every ~16 min, from which binary CPU availability intervals are derived (Rood & Lewis rule: available = host idle with no user >15 min AND CPU load <50%; FTA's relaxed variant nd07cpuB = user present but CPU load <10%). Standard FTA schema is hierarchical Platform → Node → Component → Event Trace, with event_trace (start/stop timestamps), event_state, component-type codes (CPU/mem/disk), event-type codes (availability/unavailability) and event reason codes (e.g. disk crash, CPU over
- **Failure info:** CRITICAL / weakest point. These traces do NOT contain job exit codes, job-failure labels, or diagnosed root cause. For cae06/cs06/glow06 the failure signal is host up/down as observed by Condor (a host leaving the pool = unavailability); the underlying cause (crash vs reboot vs user reclaim vs eviction vs scheduled downtime) is NOT distinguished in the standardized data. For nd07cpu the "failure"/unavailability is not even directly logged — it is INFERRED by thresholding CPU-load and idle-time telemetry, and the paper explicitly shows the same ra
- **Access:** Officially public/free from the Failure Trace Archive, historically http://fta.scem.uws.edu.au (Main.DataSets page). - **Used by:** Original FTA: Kondo, Javadi, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling Comparative Analysis of Failures in Diverse Distributed Systems," CCGrid 2010. Extended: Javadi, Kondo, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling the comparison of failure measurements and models of distributed 
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/grid-only (desktop-grid + Condor cluster availability); no AI/ML training workloads. - **Caveats:** (1) Failure cause absent: only availability/unavailability intervals — no exit codes, no reason codes populated, no crash-vs-reclaim distinction. ### Google Borg Cluster Trace 2011 (clusterdata-2011-2)
*aka:* ClusterData2011; Google trace v2; clusterdata-2011-2; Google cluster-usage traces v2
*Org:* Google (released via google/cluster-data on GitHub; hosted on Google Cloud Storage)  
*System:* A single Google Borg compute cell (one cluster), May 2011  
*Period:* 29 days starting 2011-05-01 19:00 EDT (May 2011)  
*Scale:* ~12,500 machines; ~650,000 jobs; ~25 million tasks; ~41 GB compressed on GCS
- **Contents:** Six logical tables of newline-separated CSV (SNAPPY-compressed, sharded): machine_events, machine_attributes, job_events, task_events, task_constraints, and task_usage. Records carry obfuscated job/task IDs, user (obfuscated), scheduling class (0-3, latency sensitivity), priority (0-11; free/normal/production bands), normalized resource requests (CPU/RAM/disk), and per-5-minute resource-usage measurements (mean/max CPU, memory, disk I/O, cache, plus a random 1-second CPU sample and, for part of the trace, CPI and memory-accesses-per-instruction). All resource values are normalized/scaled, not absolute.
- **Failure info:** Labels lifecycle OUTCOMES via event-type codes, not root cause and not exit codes. task_events and job_events use a "type" field: 0 SUBMIT, 1 SCHEDULE, 2 EVICT, 3 FAIL, 4 FINISH, 5 KILL, 6 LOST, 7 UPDATE_PENDING, 8 UPDATE_RUNNING. So a task/job that failed shows type=3 (FAIL); evictions (type=2), kills (type=5), normal completion (type=4), and LOST (missing-record) events are also explicitly labeled. machine_events carries ADD(0)/REMOVE(1)/UPDATE(2), where REMOVE marks a machine going out of service (failure or decommission) and re-ADD its return. CRITICAL LIMITATIONS: there is NO exit code, NO error/return status, and NO root cause or failure reason. The trace cannot distinguish an applicat
- **Access:** Public, CC-BY. Docs at https://github.com/google/cluster-data/blob/master/ClusterData2011_2.md ; data on Google Cloud Storage bucket gs://clusterdata-2011-2 (download via gsutil/gcloud). Register/announce via the google-clusterdata-discuss group.
- **Used by:** Reiss, Tumanov, Ganger, Katz, Kozuch, "Heterogeneity and dynamicity of clouds at scale: Google trace analysis," SoCC 2012 (+ CMU ISTC-CC-TR-12-101 tech report); Sharma/Rosenberg-style failure-characterization work; Rosà, Chen, Binder, "Failure Analysis and Characterization of Scheduling Jobs in Goog
- **AI vs HPC:** both (indirectly). This is a datacenter/cloud batch+service scheduler trace, NOT an HPC MPI trace and NOT AI-training-specific; there is no notion of GPU jobs, MPI ranks, or model-training runs. It is useful as the canonical "cloud/warehouse-scale" baseline against which HPC and AI failure rates are
- **Caveats:** No failure root cause, no exit/return codes — only coarse event-type outcomes; EVICT and machine REMOVE conflate preemption, over-allocation, maintenance, and hardware failure. Everything is anonymized/obfuscated (job names, users) and resource values are normalized (no absolute cores/bytes), so cross-system comparisons need care. Known data gaps: disk-time-fraction missing after ~day 14; ~0.013% 

### Google Borg cluster trace 2019 (clusterdata-2019)

*Org:* Google (Google Research / Borg team; released via google/cluster-data)

So it DOES label terminations by category: FAIL (task/job died due to a task/machine failure), EVICT (descheduled — e.g., preemption by higher priority, OOM eviction, machine going away/overcommit, disk failure), KILL (stopped by user/driver/dependent job), LOST (record missing — outcome uncertain), FINISH (normal completion). - **Access:** Public / open under CC-BY 4.0. Docs and access instructions at https://github.com/google/cluster-data/blob/master/ClusterData2019.md ; data hosted in Google BigQuery (project google.com:google-cluster-data / public dataset, access via a Google Cloud account and the googleclusterdata mailing list). Schema doc: "Google cluster-usage traces v3" PDF linked from that page.
- **Used by:** Tirmazi, Barker, Deng, Haque, Qin, Hand, Harchol-Balter, Wilkes, "Borg: the Next Generation," EuroSys 2020 (the official release/analysis paper; documents high failure rates driven by job dependencies, alloc-set usage, vertical autoscaling, per-cell variation). Also widely reused: "A Deep Dive into 
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/cloud-scheduling-adjacent, NOT AI-specific. It is a mixed-workload warehouse-scale batch+serving trace (Borg), not an ML-training-cluster trace, and it contains no GPU/accelerator fields, no model/training-job labels, and no exit codes. For the "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why" questi
- **Caveats:** Failure labels are coarse scheduler outcomes, not diagnosed faults: no exit codes, no error messages, no hardware/health telemetry, and Google explicitly maps uncertain terminations to KILL (so KILL is partly a catch-all) and folds preemption/OOM/machine-loss into EVICT. Resource metrics are normalized/obfuscated (relative units), so absolute capacities and true failure severities are not recovera
- **Confidence:** high — dataset existence, 8 cells, May 2

### Google Borg Cluster Traces (clusterdata-2011-1 / ClusterData2019)
*aka:* clusterdata-2011-1; Google 2019 cluster-usage traces v3; Borg traces; ClusterData2011_2; ClusterData2019
*Org:* Google

### Parallel Workloads Archive (PWA)
*aka:* PWA; Feitelson archive; Standard Workload Format (SWF) logs; Feitelson's Parallel Workloads Archive
*Org:* Hebrew University of Jerusalem (Dror G. Feitelson lab, School of CS & Engineering); contributions from many HPC sites (SDSC, CTC, KTH, LLNL, ANL, HPC2N, RIKEN/R  
*System:* ~40 production HPC / parallel supercomputers and clusters, including SDSC SP2/Paragon/Blue Horizon/DataStar, CTC SP2, KTH SP2, LANL CM-5, LLNL Thunder/Atlas/uBG  
*Period:* Logs span roughly 1993 to 2015+ (e.g. LANL CM-5 1994-96, CTC/KTH/SDSC SP2 mid-late 1990s, LLNL 2006-07, ANL Intrepid 2009, RICC 2010, CEA Curie 2011-12, MetaCen  
*Scale:* ~40 logs; individual logs range from thousands to tens of millions of jobs (e.g. ANL Intrepid ~68k jobs on 40,960 nodes; some logs span millions of job records)
- **Contents:** Per-job batch accounting records normalized to the 18-field Standard Workload Format (SWF): job number, submit time, wait time, run time, allocated processors, avg CPU time used, used memory, requested processors, requested time, requested memory, status (field 11), user ID, group ID, executable/application number, queue number, partition number, preceding job number, think time. Cleaned/scrubbed versions plus original raw logs and workload-header metadata provided. Also hosts derived statistical workload models.
- **Failure info:** Yes, but coarse and telemetry-level only, not diagnostic. SWF field 11 (Status) encodes job completion outcome: 0 = job failed, 1 = job completed successfully, 5 = job cancelled (by user, before or during run); other status codes (e.g. 2-4 for partial/checkpoint records, -1 unknown) appear in some logs. Critical caveats stated by the archive itself: it does NOT record exit codes, does NOT give root cause or failure reason, and the distinction between failure vs. cancellation vs. system kill is 'not necessarily accurate' because the original site logs often lacked that distinction. Many logs have status set to unknown/0-filled, and some contributed logs omit meaningful status entirely. So it 
- **Access:** Public, free download, no registration. Primary: https://www.cs.huji.ac.il/labs/parallel/workload/ (logs index at .../logs.html; SWF spec at .../swf.html). Mirror: https://jsspp.org/workload/. Users are asked to cite the archive and the JPDC 2014 paper.
- **Used by:** Feitelson, Tsafrir & Krakov, 'Experience with using the Parallel Workloads Archive,' J. Parallel & Distributed Computing 74(10):2967-2982, 2014 (DOI 10.1016/j.jpdc.2014.06.013). It is the canonical benchmark input for essentially all HPC batch-scheduling / backfilling / job-runtime-prediction resear
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only. Pure classical batch/MPI supercomputer scheduling traces; contains no GPU/accelerator, deep-learning, or AI-training workload logs, and predates the AI-cluster era for most entries. It is weak for answering 'do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why': it establishes an HPC baseline for jo
- **Caveats:** Failure labeling is the main limitation: status is coarse (fail/complete/cancel), causally uninformative, and explicitly acknowledged as imprecise; many logs have missing, zeroed, or unreliable status/memory/requested-time fields. Data-quality problems (flurries, anomalous users, non-representative periods) are documented in the 2014 paper and the archive recommends cleaned versions. No exit codes

### ALCF I/O Data Repository (Darshan logs)
*aka:* Argonne Darshan data; Mira/Theta anonymized Darshan I/O characterization logs; ANL/ALCF/TM-13/1 ALCF I/O Data Repository
*Org:* Argonne National Laboratory (ANL) / Argonne Leadership Computing Facility (ALCF); Darshan project, Mathematics and Computer Science (MCS) Division  
*System:* ALCF production systems, primarily Mira (IBM Blue Gene/Q) and Theta (Cray XC40); the broader ALCF Data Catalog also spans Intrepid, Cooley, Polaris, Aurora  
*Period:* Mira logs span roughly Jan 2014 to Dec 2020 (Mira decommissioned 2019; repository seeded from the 2013 TM-13/1 release and grown since); Theta-derived summary d  
*Scale:* ~700K anonymized per-job Darshan log files (order of hundreds of thousands of application runs); each log is one job/executable instance capturing per-file and 
- **Contents:** Per-job I/O characterization records produced by the Darshan runtime instrumentation library. Each log stores module-partitioned counters: POSIX, MPI-IO, STDIO, and (later) HDF5/PnetCDF/heatmap/DXT modules. Fields include cumulative bytes read/written, counts of read/write/open/seek/stat/mmap/fopen operations, collective vs independent vs nonblocking MPI-IO calls, aligned/sequential/consecutive/strided access counts, access-size/stride/datatype/extent histograms, open/close/first-IO/last-IO timestamps, cumulative time in I/O, file/mount/fs-type metadata, plus job metadata (nprocs, run time, start/end time, anonymized exe name, anonymized user/job IDs). File paths, user names, and executable 
- **Failure info:** CRITICAL: this dataset contains NO failure, fault, or error labeling. Darshan is strictly an I/O characterization/profiling tool — it records I/O behavior telemetry only. There are no job exit codes, no success/failure flags, no error messages, no fault classifications, and no root-cause information. A Darshan log is written when the instrumented application terminates and the runtime successfully flushes its counters, so the mere existence of a log skews toward completed runs; jobs that crash before shutdown may produce no log at all (partial/incomplete logs can occur but are not labeled as failures). Any I/O-anomaly or I/O-burst prediction study using this data must derive its own labels f
- **Access:** Public. Raw anonymized logs historically at ftp://ftp.mcs.anl.gov/pub/darshan/data/ (HTTPS mirror https://ftp.mcs.anl.gov/pub/darshan/data/ — currently returns HTTP 403 to automated fetchers). Repository landing/description at https://wordpress.cels.anl.gov/darshan/data/ . Documentation: OSTI report ANL/ALCF/TM-13/1, https://www.osti.gov/biblio/1086941 (PDF https://www.osti.gov/servlets/purl/10869
- **Used by:** Cited via the TM-13/1 ALCF I/O Data Repository report (OSTI/UNT Digital Library). Used broadly in HPC I/O ML/characterization work, e.g. "A Taxonomy of Error Sources in HPC I/O Machine Learning Models" (arXiv:2204.08180), "I/O Burst Prediction for HPC Clusters using Darshan Logs" (arXiv:2308.10311),
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only in practice. Mira/Theta workloads (2014-2020) are overwhelmingly traditional scientific/simulation HPC applications; only a small tail of later Theta-era jobs are ML/DL training runs, and the logs carry no workload-type label. Because the dataset has ZERO failure/fault information, it CANNO
- **Caveats:** (1) No failure/exit-code/root-cause labels at all — telemetry only; the single most important limitation for fault research. (2) Survivorship/coverage bias: logs exist mainly for runs where Darshan flushed at shutdown, under-representing early crashes; instrumentation coverage depends on which applications linked Darshan. (3) Anonymization hashes paths/users/exe names, so workload identity, applic
- **Confidence:** high — existence and nature confirmed vi

Darshan only instruments applications that ran with the library loaded and record their I/O behavior; it does not know or record whether a job crashed, was killed, hit a node/network/storage fault, or completed successfully. The "I/O burst" prediction in arXiv:2308.10311 is a telemetry-threshold / workload-characteristic target (magnitude of aggregate read/write rate over an interval), NOT a fault or failure event. Any failure analysis would require joinin

- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only (traditional HPC/MPI-IO application workloads on Mira; no AI/ML-training-specific labeling). - **Caveats:** (1) No failure/fault/exit-code labels at all — telemetry only. - **Contents:** Per-job I/O characterization records produced by the Darshan runtime library. Each log holds compact, per-process-aggregated I/O counters across Darshan modules: POSIX, MPI-IO, STDIO, and (where used) HDF5 and PnetCDF. Fields include bytes read/written, number of read/write/open/stat/seek/fsync operations, access-size histograms, sequential/consecutive access counts, aligned vs unaligned I/O, file counts (unique/shared/partly-shared), common access sizes, and cumulative timing (time in read/write/meta/open). Contents are purely I/O-performance telemetry (byte counts, operation counts, access patterns, timing). There is a weak, implicit-and-unreliable survivorship signal only: Darshan normally writes its log at MPI_Finalize / process exit, so hard-crashing or killed jobs often produce no log at all — but this is not a labeled or dependable failure indicator, jobs are anonymized, and no cause is ever recorded. - **AI vs HPC:** HPC-primarily, with partial AI relevance. *Scale:* Full facility job history per machine (millions of jobs/tasks); Mira RAS_EVENT files are 4-7 GB/year (~50M RAS-WARN + ~774K RAS-FATAL messages reported in downs
- **Contents:** Per-machine collection of distinct CSV data sets. Mira offers: DIM_JOB_COMPOSITE (per-job scheduler/accounting records), DARSHAN_TOTAL (Darshan-derived per-job I/O statistics), RAS_EVENT (Reliability/Availability/Serviceability event log), TASK_HISTORY, COBALT_MACHINE_RESERVATION, AUTOPERF (MPI/perf counters), DIM_MACHINE_STATUS. Non-zero exit = job failure, but there is NO categorical failure-cause label per job — cause must be inferred or joined to RAS. (2) Mira RAS_EVENT is a genuine fault/failure log with root-cause structure: SEVERITY (FATAL = leads app to fail/abort, WARN, INFO), CATEGORY (the entity that hit the error: compute card, DDR memory, link, PCI, Ethernet, InfiniBand, power module, coolant, Software_Error, ELF_Image, Job, Proc
- **Access:** Public, no login. Portal: https://reports.alcf.anl.gov/data/ (Cloudflare-protected; direct programmatic fetch is challenged, browser works). - **AI vs HPC:** Primarily HPC. Mira (BG/Q, 2013-2020) and Theta (KNL, 2017-2024) are classic CPU HPC systems with no AI/GPU-training workloads, so this record itself is HPC-only. - **Caveats:** The seed framed this as an "IO-Darshan" entry point, but the portal is much broader than Darshan I/O — the richest failure content is in RAS_EVENT (Mira) and THETA_HARDWARE_ERROR (Theta), not the Darshan tables. - **Contents:** Aggregate I/O characterization derived from Darshan logs: total data volume moved (read/write bytes), job/log counts, read vs. write ratios, Darshan module usage (POSIX, MPI-IO, STDIO, Lustre, and where present HDF5), access-size distributions, sequential/consecutive access patterns, shared vs. unique file usage, file counts, and per-executable/per-application I/O breakdowns. - **Caveats:** The public portal exposes summarized/anonymized yearly aggregates, not raw per-job Darshan logs, so per-job feature extraction (as the burst-prediction paper did) requires the underlying logs, which are obtained through ALCF rather than downloaded from this HTML site. ### Backblaze Drive Stats (Hard Drive Test Data / SMART dataset)
*aka:* Backblaze Hard Drive Test Data; Backblaze SMART dataset; Drive Stats
*Org:* Backblaze, Inc.  
*System:* Backblaze data-center storage pods (HDDs and, since ~2018, SSDs) — production cloud-storage fleet  
*Period:* April 2013 through present, ongoing. Annual dumps for 2013-2015; quarterly releases from 2016 onward (latest confirmed: Q4 2025).  
*Scale:* Very large: ~337,000 active drives as of Q4 2025 (grown from ~25k in 2013); cumulative >30 million drive-days per quarter. One row per drive per day = billions 
- **Contents:** Per-drive daily snapshot. Fixed columns: date, serial_number, model, capacity_bytes, and failure (0/1). Followed by the full set of vendor-reported S.M.A.R.T. attributes, each stored as two columns per attribute — smart_N_normalized and smart_N_raw (e.g., smart_5 reallocated sectors, smart_187 reported uncorrectable errors, smart_197 pending sectors, smart_9 power-on hours, smart_194 temperature). The set of SMART columns has expanded over the years as more attributes were captured. Attributes mix cumulative counters (reallocated sector count) and instantaneous readings (read error rate, temperature). Iceberg version adds a datacenter column in some releases.
- **Failure info:** Binary failure LABEL only, not exit codes or root cause. The `failure` column is 0 on every normal day a drive is in service and set to 1 on the LAST day the drive was operational before it was removed. A drive is counted as failed when it is taken out of service because it stopped working or SMART indicated imminent failure (e.g., failed to spin up/connect, or ran but could not sync/stay in the array). After the failure=1 row the drive disappears from subsequent files. There is NO field giving a fault code, exit status, failure mode, or root cause — the raw SMART attribute values are the only signal from which a cause might be inferred, and the label itself is operator-defined (a drive proa
- **Access:** Public / open source. Free download page: https://www.backblaze.com/cloud-storage/resources/hard-drive-test-data (per-quarter ZIP links). Terms: must cite Backblaze as source, cannot resell the raw data, derivative works allowed, use at own risk. Iceberg-table access via published read-only S3 credentials on the same page.
- **Used by:** Very widely used in disk-failure-prediction and storage-reliability literature. Examples: Backblaze's own "Using Machine Learning to Predict Hard Drive Failures" blog/analyses; Lu et al. and numerous ScienceDirect/IEEE ML papers (e.g., "Explainable time series features for hard disk drive failure pr
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-relevant but component-level, not job/workload-level. It characterizes STORAGE-DEVICE (HDD/SSD) failures in a cloud data center, not compute-node/job failures and not AI-training runs. It does NOT distinguish AI vs HPC workloads and cannot by itself answer "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and
- **Caveats:** Label is operator-defined and noisy: drives proactively removed on SMART warnings are labeled failed even without a hard crash, and drives removed for non-failure reasons (migration, cloning, upgrade) can appear as disappearances that are not failures — some early data had cloning/migration edge cases. No root-cause, no failure-mode taxonomy, no exit codes. SMART attribute semantics are vendor-spe
- **Confidence:** high — dataset existence, structure (dat

- **Access:** Public, open. GitHub: https://github.com/darshan-hpc/darshan-logs (clone + run download-large-logs.sh to fetch externally hosted large logs). Community PRs accepted for non-proprietary logs (obfuscate via darshan-convert --obfuscate).

- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only, and only weakly. It is I/O-behavior telemetry with no failure labels, so it CANNOT directly answer "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why" — there is no failure ground truth and no fault cause. - **Caveats:** Not a statistical dataset: tiny, hand-curated, non-random, no time-series coverage, no per-system labeling, no job-outcome field. *Org:* Argonne National Laboratory (ALCF)

*Scale:* 377,531 Cobalt jobs; 80,665,723 RAS messages; ~2.6M task-execution records (~7 tasks/job avg); up to 32.44 billion core-hours of usage; whole-machine (786,432 c
- **Contents:** Four joined sources keyed by Cobalt job ID. (1) RAS log: node/hardware/software events with severity (INFO/WARN/FATAL), message ID, task ID, timestamp, location, and hardware category (DDR, BQC, BQL, Software_Error, Card, Cable, Block, AC_TO_DC_PWR, Process, Coolant_Monitor, Message_Unit, Infiniband, Node_Board, Optical_Module). (2) Cobalt job-scheduling log: queued/start/end timestamps, hashed user/project, queue, wall time, runtime, nodes/cores used, allocation block location, num_of_tasks, and job exit status/exit_code. (3) Task-execution log: per-task allocation block, start/end timestamps, and — critically — exit_signal and free-text err_text describing abnormal termination (e.g., "abno
- **Failure info:** Strong, explicitly failure-labeled — one of the richest HPC failure corpora. It exposes (a) raw exit codes/exit status per job in the Cobalt log; (b) POSIX-style exit_signal values plus human-readable err_text describing the fault in the task-execution log (signal 9/11/etc. with the failing MPI rank); and (c) RAS FATAL/WARN/INFO severity with hardware fault categories. Beyond raw labels, the DSN'19 authors derive a job-failure taxonomy by joining the Cobalt exit status with task-execution signal descriptions: Timeout, Bug (SIGABRT/SIGSEGV), Kill (SIGKILL/signal 9), IO (I/O-node/file operation failures), RAS (jobs killed by system-reliability events), SIGILL/SIGTRAP/SIGFPE (signals 4/5/8), an
- **Access:** Public download from the ALCF reports portal: https://reports.alcf.anl.gov/data/mira.html (page blocks automated fetchers with HTTP 403 but is publicly reachable in a browser; the LogAider README confirms this as the canonical download location). Tooling: LogAider correlation-mining toolkit at https://github.com/disheng222/LogAider. Paper (open PDF): https://snir.cs.illinois.edu/listed/C111.pdf ; 
- **Used by:** Di, Guo, Pershey, Snir, Cappello, "Characterizing and Understanding HPC Job Failures over the 2K-day Life of IBM BlueGene/Q System," IEEE/IFIP DSN 2019 (the origin study). Also underpins the LogAider correlation tool (Di et al.), Di et al.'s earlier RAS-only Mira resilience work, and is cited/reused
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only. Mira (2013-2018) ran classical HPC simulation/analysis jobs submitted to Cobalt; there are effectively no GPU/large-model AI training workloads in this corpus. It therefore cannot directly answer "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why" from within a single system, but it is the canoni
- **Caveats:** (1) Skew: 99.4% of the 99,245 failures are user/application-caused, not system/hardware — good for studying user-job failure, weaker for hardware-fault ML unless you focus on the RAS/FATAL subset. (2) Label noise: raw Cobalt exit_codes are unreliable (nonzero often means a normal user exit); trustworthy cause requires joining the task-execution signal/err_text — no single clean ground-truth "failu
- **Confidence:** high — dataset existence, scale, time sp

### USRC VPIC restart/checkpoint particle files (LANL operational VPIC dataset)
*aka:* vpic-restart-subset-2016; C2 VPIC sample dataset; LANL operational VPIC data (LA-UR-22-31691)
*Org:* LANL (Los Alamos National Laboratory; Ultrascale Systems Research Center / lanl-future-campaign "C2" project)  
*System:* VPIC (Vector Particle-In-Cell) plasma/astrophysics simulation code; modified Harris magnetic-reconnection input deck  
*Period:* Labeled "2016" (subset name); operational tar released ~2022 (LA-UR-22-31691)  
*Scale:* Sample: 42 files, ~6 MB each, ~256K particles/timestep. Operational: 256 files, ~180 GB, ~15.7M particles/file
- **Contents:** Raw binary particle checkpoint/restart state written straight from VPIC with no header, encoding, or compression. Fixed 48-byte-per-particle records: 8B uint64 particle ID, 8B padding, 4B each x/y/z position, 4B cell index, 4B each ux/uy/uz momentum, 4B precomputed kinetic energy. Separate ion (iparticle) and electron (eparticle) files. Small "sample/subset" variant: 131,072 ions + 131,072 electrons, 21 timestep pairs (42 files) over sim steps 0-480 dumped every 24 steps, ~6 MB per file. Large operational variant: 15,728,640 particles per file, 256 files, ~180 GB total.
- **Failure info:** NONE. This is pure simulation physics telemetry (particle positions/momenta/energy). It contains no failure or fault labels, no exit codes, no error codes, no job outcomes, and no root-cause annotations. The only sense in which "restart/checkpoint" appears is the file type (VPIC checkpoint-restart state that can be reloaded to continue a run) — it is NOT a record of a failure event, and the files are not tied to any observed crash or fault. For failure-analysis purposes this dataset is a negative case: it labels nothing about failures.
- **Access:** public via anonymous LANL FTP. Confirmed live file: ftp://hpc-ftp.lanl.gov/data/operational/vpic/LA-UR-22-31691.tar (180 GB operational set). Small sample documented at github.com/lanl-future-campaign/c2-vpic-sample-dataset. The exact seed filename ftp://hpc-ftp.lanl.gov/data/operational/vpic/vpic-restart-subset-2016.tar sits in the same operational/vpic directory but was NOT independently confirm
- **Used by:** Used as a canonical "hard-to-compress" scientific dataset in lossy/lossless compression and HPC I/O research. Appears in the SDRBench Scientific Data Reduction Benchmark (arXiv:2101.03201) ecosystem and VPIC is a standard test field in SZ/SZx/cuSZ/FZ-GPU/SIMD-lossy-compression papers (e.g. arXiv:220
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only, and effectively irrelevant to the "do AI workloads fail more than HPC and why" question. It is scientific-simulation checkpoint output used for storage/compression/I/O benchmarking, with zero failure, fault, exit-code, or root-cause signal. It cannot support any failure-rate or failure-cau
- **Caveats:** (1) The dataset genuinely exists as a family of LANL operational VPIC particle files, but the precise seed filename "vpic-restart-subset-2016.tar" was not directly verified against a live FTP listing — the confirmed operational object is LA-UR-22-31691.tar and the documented sample lives in the C2 GitHub repo; the 2016 subset is plausibly an older/renamed subset in the same directory. ## Other / repositories & frameworks  (3)

### FTA — Failure Trace Archive (non-HPC subsets: lri05, deug05, pl05, websites02, ldns04, overnet03, microsoft99)
*aka:* Failure Trace Archive; FTA. Subsets: lri05, deug05 (Univ. Paris-South desktop grids); pl05 (PlanetLab all-pairs ping); websites02 (CMU HTTP probe); ldns04 (DNS); overnet03 (Overnet P2P); microsoft99 (
*Org:* Collaborative archive: TU Delft, INRIA, Univ. of Melbourne, Univ. of Heidelberg; hosted at Univ. of Western Sydney (curators Bahman Javadi, Derrick Kondo, Alexa  
*System:* Commodity / internet-scale distributed systems (NOT HPC): lri05 = LRI CS-research desktop-grid cluster (Univ. Paris-South, Entropia DCGrid); deug05 = classroom   
*Period:* Individual traces span 1999–2005 (see per-trace years in scale). Whole FTA collection spans measurements 1996–2010 across ~19+ systems.  
*Scale:* Per Table 1 of Javadi et al. 2013 (nodes, target component, period, year): lri05 = 40 nodes, CPU, ~1 month, 2005; deug05 = 40 nodes, CPU, ~1 month, 2005; pl05 (
- **Contents:** Resource-centric availability traces. The standard FTA format stores, per node/component, an alternating time series of availability and unavailability intervals (event_trace with start/end times + event_state). Data was collected almost entirely by ACTIVE PROBING: microsoft99 = ping reachability every hour; websites02 = HTTP file request every 10 min from one CMU host; pl05 = pings between all pairs of PlanetLab nodes every 15 min; ldns04 = probes with exponential inter-arrival, mean 1 hour; overnet03 = P2P probe-based measurement; lri05/deug05 = CPU/host availability of desktop-grid workers (Entropia DCGrid). Fields are node/component/platform hierarchy, event timing, and event_state (avai
- **Failure info:** CRITICAL / mostly absent for these subsets. The FTA records only binary node/host up-vs-down (availability vs unavailability) intervals INFERRED from reachability probing — a node is "unavailable" when a ping/HTTP/P2P probe fails. It does NOT expose exit codes, job-failure records, or verified root cause for any of these seven probe-derived subsets; the format is explicitly resource-centric and does not describe higher-level (job/service) failures. An unavailability interval conflates crash, reboot, network partition, maintenance, and churn — these are indistinguishable. The FTA schema DOES define optional "event reason codes" (e.g., disk crash, CPU overheating), but those are populated only
- **Access:** Public, no login, free for research. Primary site: http://fta.scem.westernsydney.edu.au (formerly http://fta.scem.uws.edu.au — the old uws.edu.au host now refuses connections; the westernsydney.edu.au host is intermittently up and its Download page returned 403/404 at check time). Also referenced via TU Delft / atlarge-research trace-archive pages. Reference paper (open): Javadi et al., JPDC 73(8)
- **Used by:** Kondo, Javadi, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling Comparative Analysis of Failures in Diverse Distributed Systems," CCGrid 2010 (Best Paper), doi:10.1109/CCGRID.2010.71. Javadi, Kondo, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling the comparison of failure measurements and model
- **AI vs HPC:** Neither AI nor HPC — these seven subsets are commodity/internet-scale systems (desktop grids, P2P, DNS, web servers, enterprise desktops, PlanetLab). The broader FTA does contain a few HPC traces (lanl05, pnnl07), but the named subsets do not. They CANNOT answer "do AI workloads fail more than HPC a
- **Caveats:** (1) Failure labels are the key gap: unavailability is inferred from probe failure, so causes (crash vs reboot vs network vs maintenance vs churn) are unrecoverable, and no exit codes/job outcomes exist. (2) Probe granularity floors event resolution — hourly (microsoft99, ldns04), 10-min (websites02), 15-min (pl05) — so short outages are missed and probe-network faults can masquerade as node faults

### FTA — SETI@home host availability trace
*aka:* seti (FTA); SETI@home availability trace; Failure Trace Archive SETI dataset; Kondo/Javadi SETI@home availability
*Org:* INRIA / University of Western Sydney (Bahman Javadi) / UC Berkeley SETI@home-BOINC (David P. Anderson); FTA curated by Derrick Kondo, Alexandru Iosup, Dick Epem  
*System:* SETI@home volunteer/desktop-grid (Internet-distributed BOINC hosts), packaged in the Failure Trace Archive  
*Period:* Roughly 1.5 years of measurement, approximately Dec 2007–2009 (BOINC/SETI@home client-reported availability); exact window approximate — verify against the trac  
*Scale:* ~230,000 Internet hosts (volunteer PCs); one of the largest single availability traces in the FTA
- **Contents:** Per-host CPU/host availability intervals: timestamped transitions between "available" and "unavailable" states for each of ~230K hosts, collected by the BOINC client (host powered on and running BOINC, with CPU free for the project). Normalized into the FTA relational schema (platform, node, component, event_trace, event_type, event_start/stop tables) so availability is expressed as start/stop availability events per node. No workload/job records — purely host availability/uptime-downtime.
- **Failure info:** TELEMETRY / AVAILABILITY ONLY — this is the critical caveat. The trace records binary host availability state transitions (available ↔ unavailable), which the FTA treats as "failure/repair" events (a host going unavailable = a failure event, coming back = a repair). It does NOT expose exit codes, error codes, or any diagnosed root cause. Crucially, unavailability lumps together fundamentally different causes — machine power-off, reboot, network disconnection, user reclaiming the CPU, and voluntary departure/churn — with no field distinguishing them. So it labels WHEN a host becomes unavailable and for how long (inter-event times, availability/unavailability durations), but not WHY. This is d
- **Access:** Public historically via the Failure Trace Archive, http://fta.scem.uws.edu.au/index.php?n=Main.DataSets (University of Western Sydney host). NOTE: that host is currently unreachable (connection refused) as of 2026-07 — the FTA site appears down/defunct. Underlying trace also distributed via the BOINC/SETI@home availability-trace releases (Kondo/Anderson). Paper DOI for provenance: 10.1109/CCGRID.2
- **Used by:** Kondo, Javadi, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling Comparative Analysis of Failures in Diverse Distributed Systems," CCGrid 2010 (DOI 10.1109/CCGRID.2010.71); Javadi, Kondo, Iosup, Epema, "The Failure Trace Archive: Enabling the comparison of failure measurements and models of distrib
- **AI vs HPC:** Neither AI nor datacenter-HPC — it is volunteer/desktop-grid (Internet-distributed computing). Distributed-systems adjacent but a different failure regime: host churn and voluntary availability, driven by end-user behavior, not job faults, hardware failures, or GPU/interconnect errors. LOW direct va
- **Caveats:** (1) Availability-only: no root cause, no exit/error codes, no fault taxonomy — unavailability conflates shutdown, reboot, CPU contention, and voluntary churn. (2) Measurement bias toward volunteer desktop PCs behind consumer networks, not server/datacenter hardware. (3) Original FTA host (fta.scem.uws.edu.au) is currently down; access/persistence uncertain — may need Wayback Machine or contacting 
- **Confidence:** high (dataset and its FTA provenance/pap

### RoWD (Rogue Workload Detector) framework — releases the SCRIPT-AI dataset
*aka:* ROWD; "Rowdy"; SCRIPT-AI (the dataset it releases); Raikon = RIKEN (misreading)
*Org:* RIKEN R-CCS + University of Bologna (Italy)  
*System:* Supercomputer Fugaku (RIKEN Center for Computational Science)  
*Period:* Jobs drawn from Fugaku operation 2021–2025 (evaluation window). Paper published Jan 2026.  
*Scale:* Framework evaluated on ~50K previously unseen Fugaku jobs; the released SCRIPT-AI dataset is a set of annotated job submission scripts (subset of Fugaku's job s
- **Contents:** RoWD is a plug-in framework that ingests HPC job-submission scripts and associated job metadata and classifies workloads to flag "rogue" (unauthorized/malicious/misuse) jobs. Its released artifact, SCRIPT-AI, is described as "the first dataset of annotated job scripts labeled with workload characteristics" — primarily an AI-vs-non-AI workload classification label derived from Slurm/job-script content and metadata. Signals are the job SCRIPT text and submission metadata, not runtime hardware telemetry.
- **Failure info:** CRITICAL: This resource does NOT label failures, faults, exit codes, or root cause. RoWD is a security-classification tool; its labels concern WORKLOAD TYPE (is a job an AI workload? is it a rogue/misused job?), not job outcome or reliability. SCRIPT-AI annotates job scripts with workload characteristics (e.g., AI vs non-AI), not termination status or fault cause. There is no failure/exit-code/root-cause signal here. (For actual Fugaku job exit codes and outcomes, the RELATED but separate dataset by the same lead author — F-DATA, Zenodo 11467483 — is the one that carries exit codes; RoWD/SCRIPT-AI does not.)
- **Access:** Paper: restricted (paywalled) — Future Generation Computer Systems, DOI 10.1016/j.future.2026.01.026, ScienceDirect S0167739X26000269. Also indexed on ResearchGate (pub 400014668). SCRIPT-AI dataset release location (Zenodo/GitHub) not confirmed from the public abstract; claimed released alongside the paper. Access = on-request / check paper's data-availability statement.
- **Used by:** Antici, Domke, Bartolini, Kiziltan, Matsuoka (Univ. Bologna + RIKEN R-CCS) — the authors themselves. Lead author Francesco Antici; same group produced the related F-DATA Fugaku workload dataset. Too new (Jan 2026) for external citing works yet.
- **AI vs HPC:** Both (AI vs HPC distinction is literally its subject) — BUT for FAILURE analysis it is NOT useful. RoWD/SCRIPT-AI can tell you WHICH Fugaku jobs are AI vs traditional HPC (that classification is exactly what it provides, F1 95%), so it could label the AI-vs-HPC axis. However it carries no failure/ou
- **Caveats:** Disambiguation confirmed: RoWD is a RIKEN/Bologna HPC-SECURITY tool, NOT a Bangalore/Hari fault-prediction dataset and no University of Alabama link. "Raikon" = RIKEN. It is a framework, not primarily a dataset, though it releases SCRIPT-AI. Gaps: could not fetch full text (ScienceDirect 403 / DOI resolver 404 at query time) or ResearchGate (403); SCRIPT-AI's exact size, format, schema, and public
- **Confidence:** high — the paper's existence, title, ven

# Additions from completeness critic (2026-06-25)

These were surfaced by a follow-up completeness-critic pass after the main run hit its session limit. They are datasets the interrupted run did not discover. Where "paper-stats-only" appears, the failure numbers live in the paper but no raw trace was released.

## AI / GPU-training failure sources

### Unicron (Alibaba)
*aka:* "Unicron: Economizing Self-Healing LLM Training at Scale" — arXiv:2401.00134
- **Contents:** failure characterization from Alibaba production LLM-training clusters plus a self-healing manager.
- **Failure info:** aggregate root-cause stats — 43.4% job-failure rate, ~37% hardware, ~73% restart-remediable. No raw trace.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. One of the cleanest published "AI jobs fail at rate X, hardware-dominated" numbers.

### From Detection to Recovery (504× B200 cluster)
*Org:* Lablup / SKT / NVIDIA Korea — arXiv:2605.09370
- **Contents:** 55 days Prometheus telemetry (751 metrics) + 73 days logs, 224 sessions, 63-node / 504-GPU B200 cluster.
- **Failure info:** 10 XID-classified GPU failures with precursor analysis, node-exclusion patterns, 523 checkpoint-I/O events. Tables, no raw dump.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Modern B200 production cluster with explicit XID taxonomy; comparable to the Fire-Flyer / DGX Xid entries.

### SuperBench (Microsoft Azure A100 fleet)
*aka:* USENIX ATC'24 best paper; TOCS'25 — arXiv:2402.06194
- **Contents:** 2 years of Azure GPU-cluster validation, 24k+ A100s over 90 days.
- **Failure info:** defect-node rate (10.36% flagged defective), gray-failure/degradation stats, MTBI gains. No raw data.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Strong "AI GPU nodes fail far more than generic cloud nodes" evidence.

### L4 (Microsoft "Platform-X")
*aka:* FSE 2025, "Diagnosing Large-scale LLM Training Failures via Automated Log Analysis" — arXiv:2503.20263
- **Contents:** 428 real LLM-training failures (May 2023–Apr 2024) + root-cause study and log-diagnosis framework.
- **Failure info:** root-cause distribution (hardware + user dominant), failure-log taxonomy. Underlying logs proprietary.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Independent large-N (428) production LLM failure root-cause breakdown.

### Fault-Tolerant HSDP at 100,000 GPUs (Meta)
*aka:* Salpekar et al., arXiv:2602.00277 (Jan 2026)
- **Contents:** fault-tolerance design + interruption telemetry from a recurring ~32K-GPU job (framed toward 100K). Distinct from the cataloged Llama-3 405B and Meta "Revisiting Reliability".
- **Failure info:** 2.3 interruptions per 1,000 servers/day, 78% hardware-caused, 95–97% effective training time.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Newest Meta interruption rate at extreme scale, normalized per server-day.

### Minder (ByteDance)
*aka:* NSDI 2025, "Faulty Machine Detection for Large-scale Distributed Model Training" — arXiv:2411.01791
- **Contents:** 150 runtime fault instances over 9 months, up to ~10,000 Ampere GPUs.
- **Failure info:** fault-instance descriptions + detection performance. Dataset not released.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Real AI-cluster fault population with counts.

### BLOOM / BigScience training chronicles (176B)
*URL:* github.com/bigscience-workshop/bigscience (train/tr11-176B-ml/chronicles.md)
- **Contents:** day-by-day operational logbook on Jean Zay (~384 A100s) plus Slurm scripts.
- **Failure info:** dated narrative events (~1–2 GPU hardware failures/week, dataloader deadlock, disk-space outages, a loss spike). Human-readable, not machine-parseable labels.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Rare fully-open academic-consortium logbook.

### GLM-130B training logs (Tsinghua / Zhipu)
*URL:* github.com/zai-org/GLM-130B (logs/main-log-en.md)
- **Contents:** public training log + TensorBoard, Dec 2021–Jul 2022.
- **Failure info:** dated hardware failures, NaN-loss/data issues, FP16 instabilities + interventions.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Second fully-open large-model logbook alongside BLOOM.

### Imbue 70B "from bare metal" report
*URL:* imbue.com/blog/70b-infrastructure
- **Contents:** bring-up/burn-in of a 4,092× H100 / 511-host cluster + open health-check scripts.
- **Failure info:** enumerated bring-up defects (PCIe link-width degradation on ~25% of hosts, ECC/NVLink errors, firmware mismatches). Categorical inventory, not a time series.
- **Access:** public report + open scripts.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Startup-scale H100 defect profile, contrast to hyperscaler numbers.

### LLM360 (Amber / CrystalCoder / K2)
*URL:* llm360.ai ; huggingface.co/LLM360
- **Contents:** full training logs, checkpoints, W&B curves for open 7B–65B runs.
- **Failure info:** loss-spike events (benign vs malignant, K2-Spike checkpoints); light on hardware faults.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI. Open training-dynamics data for the software/instability side of the failure split.

### TPUv4 Resiliency at Scale (Google)
*aka:* Zu et al., NSDI 2024
- **Contents:** TPUv4 pod availability, OCS reconfiguration, fail-stop + SDC handling at 10k-chip scale.
- **Failure info:** availability (99.98%), goodput (>97%), failure-mode discussion. No raw data.
- **Access:** paper-stats-only.
- **AI vs HPC:** AI (accelerator/ASIC). Reliability beyond NVIDIA.

### Smaller AI silent-data-corruption studies
- **LLM-PRISM** (arXiv:2604.10390) — precision resilience over 7,664 runs, simulated faults. paper-stats.
- **Exploring SDC as a Reliability Challenge in LLM Training** (arXiv:2604.00726) — public artifact at github.com/aaltenbernd/llm-sdc-training.
- **Understanding SDC in LLM Training** (ACL 2025, arXiv:2502.12340) — paper-stats.
- **The Anatomy of SDC: GPU Error Pattern Study** (arXiv:2605.04213) — gate-level fault injection.

## Silent Data Corruption / memory field studies (the "invisible failure" class RAS logs miss)

### Silent Data Corruptions at Scale (Meta)
*aka:* Dixit 2021; Facebook SDC study
*Org:* Meta (Facebook)
*System:* Meta production CPU fleet
*Period:* 18+ months
*Scale:* hundreds of thousands of machines
- **Contents:** Case study of a real SDC and a taxonomy of manufacturing defect types that cause silent corruption.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. Prevalence, not shares: "hundreds of CPUs detected for these errors" across "hundreds of thousands of machines in our fleet". No failure-class breakdown, no released dataset.
- **Access:** public, arXiv:2102.11245
- **Used by:** Cited in the paper's CPU-side subsection as one of the rates-only SDC trio.
- **AI vs HPC:** both (CPU side)
- **Caveats:** Order-of-magnitude prevalence only; exact rates withheld.

### Cores That Don't Count (Google)
*aka:* Hochschild HotOS'21; mercurial cores; CEEs
*Org:* Google
*System:* Google production fleet
*Period:* not stated
*Scale:* large fleet
- **Contents:** Call-to-action describing corrupt execution errors (CEEs) and mercurial cores.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. "For business reasons, we are unable to reveal exact CEE rates, but we observe on the order of a few mercurial cores per several thousand machines - similar to the rate reported by Facebook". No shares, no dataset.
- **Access:** public, ACM DL / sigops
- **Used by:** Cited in the paper's CPU-side subsection.
- **AI vs HPC:** both (CPU side)
- **Caveats:** Rates explicitly withheld by the authors.

### Understanding Silent Data Corruptions in a Large Production CPU Population (Alibaba)
*aka:* Wang SOSP'23; Farron
*Org:* Alibaba Cloud + Tsinghua
*System:* Alibaba Cloud fleet, 28 data centers
*Period:* 32 months, processors deployed since 2017
*Scale:* over 1,000,000 processors
- **Contents:** Large-scale SDC testing campaign with micro-architecture, timing and temperature analysis, plus the Farron mitigation system.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. Observation 1: "In overall, 3.61 (per 10,000) of the CPUs are identified to cause SDCs in our study". Observation 3: "The failure rate does not decrease with newer chips". Population rate, not failure-class shares. No dataset.
- **Access:** public, ACM DL (10.1145/3600006.3613149)
- **Used by:** Cited in the paper's CPU-side subsection; the most precise SDC population rate available.
- **AI vs HPC:** both (CPU side)
- **Caveats:** Rate is per-CPU prevalence; cannot be converted to a share of job failures.

### SEVI: Silent Data Corruption of Vector Instructions in Hyper-Scale Datacenters
*aka:* SEVI; ASPLOS'26
*Org:* Meta + Carnegie Mellon
*System:* Meta hyperscale CPU fleet
*Period:* 78 trillion test rounds / 14 billion CPU-seconds
*Scale:* hyperscale
- **Contents:** Instruction- and application-level analysis of vector-instruction SDCs plus an ABFT-based detection mechanism.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY for our purposes. Reports shares BY INSTRUCTION TYPE (FMA over 92% of observed SDCs; 98.5% affect a single vector lane), not by cluster failure class, so it cannot populate a failure-class column.
- **Access:** public, ACM DL (10.1145/3779212.3790217)
- **Used by:** Not cited in the paper.
- **AI vs HPC:** both (CPU side)
- **Caveats:** Different basis entirely: instruction type, not failure cause.

### DRAM Errors in the Wild: A Large-Scale Field Study
*aka:* Schroeder/Pinheiro/Weber SIGMETRICS'09
*Org:* Google
*System:* Google server fleet
*Period:* 2.5 years
*Scale:* majority of Google's fleet
- **Contents:** Field study of DRAM correctable and uncorrectable error behavior by chip density, technology and DIMM age.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. "25,000 to 70,000 errors per billion device hours per Mbit and more than 8% of DIMMs affected by errors per year". Device rates, no failure-class shares, no dataset.
- **Access:** public, author copy
- **Used by:** Not cited in the paper; the canonical DRAM field baseline.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/CPU side
- **Caveats:** Pre-dates the AI-cluster era; memory technology has moved on.

### Revisiting Memory Errors in Large-Scale Production Data Centers
*aka:* Meza DSN'15; Facebook DRAM study
*Org:* Facebook + CMU
*System:* Facebook server fleet
*Period:* 14 months
*Scale:* billions of device days
- **Contents:** Modern DRAM error study with modeling of new trends and an evaluation of page offlining.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. Memory error incidence and modeling only; no breakdown of system or job failures by cause category. No dataset released.
- **Access:** public, paper
- **Used by:** Not cited in the paper.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC/CPU side
- **Caveats:** Memory subsystem only.

## DOE / vendor GPU reliability studies (HPC-side accelerator baselines, all paper-stats)

### Experiences Detecting Defective Hardware in Exascale Supercomputers
*aka:* Frontier defect screening; Hagerty SC'23
*Org:* ORNL
*System:* OLCF Frontier (MI250X)
*Period:* June 2023 screening month
*Scale:* 9,408 compute nodes
- **Contents:** Two strategies for finding hardware-level faults: Slurm scheduler scavenging and an enforced weekly per-node screen.
- **Failure info:** RATES-ONLY. Reports "five hardware defects in Frontier" found during June 2023 across 9,408 nodes. This is a diagnostic screening programme, not a production failure characterization; the 19-of-24 and 5-of-24 detection counts were rejected as failure shares by the three-pass verification. No dataset released.
- **Access:** public, OSTI 2224160
- **Used by:** Feeds the Frontier column's justification for staying empty in both share matrices. Resolves ledger.md section 4 item 2.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC
- **Caveats:** One screening month; detections are not production failure shares.

### Monitoring Large Scale Supercomputers: A Case Study with the Lassen Supercomputer
*aka:* Patki IEEE Cluster 2021; CSM monitoring study
*Org:* LLNL + IBM
*System:* LLNL Lassen
*Period:* multiple years
*Scale:* over 1.4 million jobs, heterogeneous nodes
- **Contents:** Longitudinal study of a first-of-its-kind dataset collected by IBM Cluster System Management (CSM), with a power-management case study.
- **Failure info:** DUPLICATE. Corrected 2026-09-20 after reading the source: this is a MONITORING and POWER study, not the "Sierra-class GPU failure/repair MTBF/MTTR" study an earlier prose bullet described. Its released data is the same Lassen job/energy corpus already held as the LAST entry.
- **Access:** public, OSTI 1820011
- **Used by:** Collapses into the LAST Lassen job/energy entry.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC
- **Caveats:** Earlier catalog description was wrong; corrected here.

### Examining Failures and Repairs on Supercomputers with Multi-GPU Compute Nodes
*aka:* Taherin DSN 2021; Tsubame failure logs
*Org:* LLNL + Northeastern University
*System:* TSUBAME-2 and TSUBAME-3 (Tokyo Tech GSIC)
*Period:* T2 Jan 2012 - Aug 2013; T3 May 2017 - Feb 2020
*Scale:* T2 1,408 nodes / 3 K20X per node; T3 4 P100 per node
- **Contents:** Two failure logs with time of failure, time to recovery and failure category per event, plus the analysis tooling.
- **Failure info:** COLUMN-CAUSE with a released dataset: "Our analysis tool and failure logs are available open-source". T2 = 897 failures, T3 = 338 failures. Source-stated shares of all logged failures: GPU 44.37% and CPU 1.78% on T2; software 50.59% and GPU 27.81% on T3; GPU-driver problems 42.7% of the 171 T3 software root loci. Table III gives multi-GPU involvement: T2 roughly 70% of GPU failures affect more than one GPU, T3 over 92% affect only one.
- **Access:** public dataset, doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.4606221
- **Used by:** Supplies the TSUBAME-2 and TSUBAME-3 columns of the cause-attributed map and the correlated-failure and proactive-testing findings.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC (GPU-dense, bridges to the AI side)
- **Caveats:** Category taxonomies differ between T2 and T3; some shares are bar-chart reads.

### Understanding the Effects of DRAM Correctable Error Logging at Scale
*aka:* CE logging overhead study; IEEE Cluster 2021
*Org:* DOE laboratory authors
*System:* simulation of extreme-scale workloads
*Period:* n/a
*Scale:* n/a
- **Contents:** Simulation of the relationship between correctable-error recovery and logging activity and application performance.
- **Failure info:** NO-FAILURE-INFO. A simulation and modeling study, not a measurement of real failures. The one rate it quotes (correctable errors roughly 20x uncorrectable) is taken from other work.
- **Access:** public, OSTI 1881688
- **Used by:** Not cited in the paper.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC
- **Caveats:** Simulation only; contributes no observed failure data.

## Supercomputer job-outcome & telemetry datasets (mostly downloadable)

### SURF Lisa "Generic and ML Workloads in an HPC Datacenter" ⭐
*aka:* ICPADS'24; "How Do ML Jobs Fail in Datacenters?"; arXiv:2409.08949 — DOI 10.5281/zenodo.13685426
- **Contents:** ~10 months Slurm traces + ~90-attribute node telemetry from Lisa (338 nodes, ~85% CPU "generic", ~15% GPU "ML").
- **Failure info:** per-job Slurm exit state, failure rates, energy wasted on failures, GPU thermal-limit events; explicitly splits ML vs generic jobs.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** both. Probably the single most on-point dataset — generic-HPC and ML jobs side-by-side on one machine with exit labels.

### NREL Eagle HPC Jobs
*DOI:* 10.7799/1722950 ; github.com/NREL/eagle-jobs
- **Contents:** 11M+ Slurm jobs (Nov 2018–Feb 2023), scheduling + resource + power fields (Parquet).
- **Failure info:** Slurm exit states incl. FAILED, NODE_FAIL, CANCELLED, TIMEOUT, OUT_OF_MEMORY.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. Large clean job-outcome baseline for generic-HPC failure rates.

### NREL Kestrel HPC Jobs
*URL:* OEDI/OSTI (companion to Eagle), ~11M rows
- **Contents:** anonymized per-Slurm-job records, resource + energy metrics; GPU-dense machine.
- **Failure info:** same Slurm state family incl. NODE_FAIL.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. Pairs with Eagle for a within-site generational comparison.

### IN2P3 Computing Center 2024 Workload Dataset
*URL:* arXiv:2606.05914
- **Contents:** 44M jobs, full-year 2024, up to 312 machines, incl. memory-usage metrics.
- **Failure info:** per-job execution/exit records (job-outcome level, no RAS console labels).
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. Recent European throughput-cluster baseline.

### ALCF Cobalt workload + RAS traces (IIT / Zhiling Lan) ⭐
*URL:* cs.iit.edu/~lan/cobalt.html
- **Contents:** Cobalt scheduler traces + RAS event logs from ANL Intrepid and Mira.
- **Failure info:** job termination info PLUS separate RAS/hardware-fault logs — a rare pairing of job outcome with machine faults.
- **Access:** public download (with citation).
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. Lets you correlate job failure with underlying node RAS events.

### Two Sigma cluster trace (via CMU ATLAS)
*URL:* pdl.cmu.edu/ATLAS
- **Contents:** 1300-node commercial analytics cluster, ~9 months 2016–2017. (Mustang/Trinity portions of ATLAS are already in the main catalog; Two Sigma is the incremental piece.)
- **Failure info:** job_status field (JOBEND / JOBFAIL / JOBCANCEL) + wallclock_limit for timeout inference.
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** commercial analytics. Same format as the LANL traces.

### Frontier Energy / Waste-Heat Dataset (OLCF)
*DOI:* 10.6084/m9.figshare.24391240 ; paper 10.1038/s41597-024-03913-w
- **Contents:** Frontier data-center power demand + waste-heat/cooling facility measurements (8–30 MW).
- **Failure info:** none (power/thermal telemetry only).
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. The main public Frontier operational dataset; energy/thermal envelope baseline.

### Adastra MI250 power dataset (CINES/GENCI)
*DOI:* 10.5281/zenodo.14007065
- **Contents:** 15 days of node/CPU/memory power on the AMD MI250 exascale-class Adastra.
- **Failure info:** none (power only).
- **Access:** public download.
- **AI vs HPC:** HPC. Recent AMD-GPU European power telemetry.

### LAST — Lassen job/energy dataset (LLNL)
*URL:* data-science.llnl.gov/open-data-initiative (cited in arXiv:2508.20016)
- **Contents:** ~1.4M jobs, allocation + job-step disposition + accumulated energy per job (V100/Power9).
- **Failure info:** job-step disposition (completion/outcome), not RAS console logs.
- **Access:** public-but-verify (direct DOI not firmly confirmed; possibly on-request).

*Borderline:* Blue Waters workload/accounting via NCSA data-sets page (Torque/PBS outcomes, on-request via Globus; distinct from the cataloged LogDiver/K20X entries).

## I/O and storage datasets

### DKRZ "Mistral" job I/O dataset (Betke & Kunkel)
*aka:* mistral-io-datasets; "10^6 jobs" I/O dataset — Zenodo 4478960 (paper)
- **Contents:** per-job/per-node/per-filesystem Lustre I/O time series (13 metrics, 5s sampling), ~99 days 2019, ~1M jobs raw / ~339k curated on Mistral (DKRZ).
- **Failure info:** NONE. I/O telemetry only, curated to completed jobs; no exit codes or causes.

- **AI vs HPC:** HPC-only, I/O-only. Cannot answer the AI-vs-HPC question (no workload-type and no failure labels).
- **Confidence:** high on characterization; medium on raw-data availability.

*URL:* arXiv:1801.07624
- **Contents:** Slurm scheduler/accounting records (submit/start/end, node counts, wait times, partitions, job state/exit).
- **Failure info:** carries job state/exit fields — the failure-relevant Mistral dataset (distinct from the I/O one).
- **Access:** see paper.

### SNIA Reliability Traces: Alibaba NVMe Fail-Slow & Fail-Stop
*URL:* iotta.snia.org/traces/reliability
- **Contents:** Fail-Slow (degraded NVMe, ~11 months) + Fail-Stop (complete SSD failures, ~4 months).
- **Failure info:** explicit fail-slow (degradation) and fail-stop (hard) device events.

### Alibaba SSD SMART logs (dcbrain)
*URL:* github.com/alibaba-edu/dcbrain/tree/master/ssd_smart_logs
